# CORE-CAP-SPECIALTY — consolidated source profiles

Uses an existing registered canonical owner and preserves all prior addenda. All owners already have canonical specifications. Every clause below applies only to its listed source profiles; it is not a universal clinical rule. No new service, clinical approval or implementation readiness.

## diagnostic-specialty-assessment

Existing Specialty owns this source-scoped domain workflow and longitudinal projection. Original condition, laboratory, imaging, medication, prescription, encounter, immunization and device owners retain authoritative facts and clinical actions. Repeated names are profile variants, not interchangeable clinical protocols or independently deployed services. Hearing-loss etiologic assessment and vestibular/balance evaluation are multidisciplinary workflows, not a single assay or imaging study.

Acceptance additions: Preserve every source-specific requirement with original ID, source line, acquisition/effective time, author and revision. Distinguish reported, observed, suspected, confirmed, inferred and missing states. Keep pediatric/guardian/adolescent confidentiality and specialty transitions explicit. Treatment, escalation, dosing, device settings and invasive procedures require qualified clinical decisions; no source automation phrase grants execution authority. Preserve hearing phenotype, laterality, age, congenital infection, medication exposure, genetic/family context and rehabilitation needs.

## registry-specialty-projections

Specialty owns disease, development, reproductive, transplant and functional longitudinal registry projections. Each source variant retains patient/donor/recipient/family identity and clinical context; conditions, laboratory, medications and encounters retain authoritative facts. A shared registry profile is not a single clinical protocol or permission to merge people.

Acceptance additions: Preserve source identity, source-specific fields, verification, chronology and correction lineage. Test unknown versus absent states, wrong-patient/tenant binding, stale or revoked access, conflicting reports and attributed human disposition. Clinical actions and sensitive pediatric/guardian disclosure require separately approved workflows; no inferred automation or compatibility approval.

## precision-specialty-profiles

Existing Specialty owns this source-scoped domain workflow and longitudinal projection. Original condition, laboratory, imaging, medication, prescription, encounter, immunization and device owners retain authoritative facts and clinical actions. Repeated names are profile variants, not interchangeable clinical protocols or independently deployed services. Precision denotes source-specific personalization/genomic/phenotype context, not proven utility or a new service. Clinical evidence and licensed knowledge remain versioned and patient-applicable.

Acceptance additions: Preserve every source-specific requirement with original ID, source line, acquisition/effective time, author and revision. Distinguish reported, observed, suspected, confirmed, inferred and missing states. Keep pediatric/guardian/adolescent confidentiality and specialty transitions explicit. Treatment, escalation, dosing, device settings and invasive procedures require qualified clinical decisions; no source automation phrase grants execution authority. Preserve genomic uncertainty and family-specific consent; a biomarker, predictor or matched cohort cannot alone establish treatment eligibility.
